Since the outputscale is lower now, maybe the model is more confident in its predictions. However, since f_min hasn't improved, there's a need to explore further.

Alternatively, since the outputscale is lower than the previous step (3254 to 942), maybe the model is more confident now. In that case, Expected Improvement (EI) could work better now. EI focuses on regions that are likely to improve over the current best, which might be effective if the model is more confident. 
But since EI was used before (N=47) and didn't work, maybe UCB is better. UCB was used at N=48 and 43, but given the lower outputscale now, it might be a better time to try UCB again to balance between exploring areas with higher uncertainty and exploiting the current best.

Hartmann6 (scaled variant): The persistent f_min (~900) and extreme f_range suggest a synthetic benchmark function with a narrow global minimum surrounded by high-value regions, typical of the Hartmann family. The high dimensionality (D=5), rugged landscape (large outputscale fluctuations), and the optimizer's struggle to improve f_min align with Hartmann's characteristics. The Matern 5/2 kernel with ARD effectively models its mixed smoothness and relevance across dimensions, while the acquisition function shifts (TS → UCB → PosMean) reflect the balance needed between exploration in early stages and exploitation near convergence. The function's design likely includes scaling to elevate the minimum to ~900 and amplify variance, creating a challenging test case for Bayesian optimization.
